### 12.18 D64 — a review whose answer's statements do not fit lists none of them (`REG-D-153`; `REG-F-626`; `REG-D-146`; D52)

64. **D64, DECIDED (2026-11-11) — when a work product's review, listing every statement, would pass a bound, it lists none of the answer's statements and asks the decision model none of them; the checks and the account's statements are listed and asked as today; the fingerprint's move is acknowledged with no reviewer call; and the run report states the rule.** This is the lead's design of `REG-D-153`. The lead's decisions in it, each named: the variant sentence's words; that either bound brings it in; that a review still past a bound is refused with today's statement; and the report's count, the lead's addition. ~~The build is OWED (package L9).~~ Built in package L9 (`f99a2713`, 2026-11-12); its test of an answer's statement takes a source that is `answer` or starts `answer.` (the build's reading of *"whose source begins `answer`"* below).
    - **What changes.** `reviewPrompt` (`reasoning-review-validator.ts`) cuts the checks and the statements as today and measures the prompt that lists every one with every score at `0.00`, as today (D52). Within both bounds, the prompt is today's, byte for byte. Past a bound, when the cut holds statements of the answer (those cut from the region under the subject line *"The WORK PRODUCT to review"*, whose source begins `answer`; `statementsOf`, `review-cuts.ts`), it measures again with those statements removed and the block's second line the variant sentence below. Within both bounds, the decision model is asked the checks and the remaining statements, each request as measured (`REG-D-146` (2)), and that prompt is sent, checked again as sent. Otherwise the review is refused as today, with the statement for the prompt that lists every statement, byte for byte.
    - **The variant sentence**, in that prompt alone and in place of `INDICATIONS_SAYS` (the lead's words): `Before this review, a decision model was asked about this work one question at a time: each check below on its own, and each statement from the producer's account on its own. The statements of the producer's answer were not asked about and are not listed, since listing them would make this prompt too long; the answer itself is shown above. Each number is the model's probability, from 0 to 1. These are potential indications to consider as you judge every check and every claim against the evidence.` Its first sentence drops `answer and ` from today's; its last two are today's. Only an answer shown whole has statements cut, so the answer it names is shown whole.
    - **Where it reaches.** A work product's review alone: a process result's, a part's and a declared policy's subjects carry other lines before their regions, so their cuts hold no statement of an answer, and each is refused past a bound as today.
    - **What stays.** D52's bounds: 9,500 of growth, 700 a quoted statement, and 35,500 whole (`REG-D-149`). The cut. Every prompt within both bounds, so no prompt of case set v13 moves. D52's six refusal probes, each still refused with its statement, byte for byte: five hold no statement of an answer, and `W-ACCOUNT-BREAKS`'s one, 87 characters, is 143 characters of its 9,781 of growth as the block lists it, and the variant sentence is 156 characters longer than today's, so without it the growth is 9,794, still past 9,500 (the lead's arithmetic, with an 18-character fence, which the build checks).
    - **What it moves.** The section's fixed text gains the variant sentence, which the fingerprint's pointer component holds whole (`REVIEW_SECTION_TEXT`; `decision-model-pointer.ts`). D64's branch is reached by a witness the fingerprint hashes, rendered with no new decision-model record (`REG-D-152`): the probes the product sends render from the records, so a new one would need a record, while the pointer component renders its witnesses against a server held in memory. The witness is a work product whose answer, shown whole, has statements that pass the growth bound when listed, its account small. So `REVIEWER_FINGERPRINT_TODAY` is re-pinned, and the qualification record owes, as D56's did, RESCORED lines re-stamping D53's evaluations under the new fingerprint (`rescoredLines`), an ACKNOWLEDGED line whose `reason` quotes `REG-D-153` (a move no case prompt shows; how the reviewer judges a prompt that lists none of an answer's statements is unmeasured), and an ESCALATION line for each material failure the RESCORED lines re-stamp, citing the finding D53's line cites. No reviewer call. `reviewer:qual:check` GREEN.
    - **The run report.** Its list a "Yes" always states gains: `REG-D-153: a work product's review whose prompt, listing every statement, would pass a bound lists none of the answer's statements and asks the decision model none of them; the reviewer reads the answer itself, its indications covering the checks and the producer's account; the sponsor accepted this without re-measurement.` And, the lead's addition, beside its count of prompts past 31,867: how many of the initial reviewer calls whose prompt the record holds carry the variant sentence.
    - **Its tests, each red at HEAD.** The fourth run's case in shape, a work product whose answer, shown whole, has statements that pass the growth bound when listed, its account small: refused at HEAD; sent after, its block listing the checks and the account's statements alone under the variant sentence, the decision model asked those alone. A refusal kept: an input still past a bound without the answer's statements, refused with today's statement for the full list, byte for byte. The whole bound's case where an input can reach it, or the build says why none can. Controls, green at HEAD and after: a prompt within both bounds, byte for byte as today; a process result's review past a bound, refused as today. The report's new line verbatim and its count. **Its checks.** The ledger's mutants (the branch's condition, the removal, the variant sentence, the refusal kept); `reviewer:qual:check` GREEN with the fingerprint re-pinned and the record's lines appended; `bun run gate:fast` green.

### 12.19 D65 — an edit changes only the prompt fields it passes, and Publish refuses a model-run type whose prompt cannot run (`REG-D-154`; `REG-F-627`; `REG-F-508`, remedy 1)

65. **D65, DECIDED (2026-11-12) — the agent's edit of a unit type changes only the prompt fields the call passes, and Publish refuses a PWA with a type declaring MODEL whose prompt its unit's step could not run, naming each such type; no text any model reads changes.** This is the lead's design of `REG-D-154` (1) and (2). The lead's decisions in it, each named: an empty text is read as not passed, as today; the template's version is kept as stored; the check sits in `PublishPwa`; it is decided by the code that decides it when the step runs; and the refusal's words. ~~The build is OWED (package L10).~~ Built in package L10 (`25a0dff8`, 2026-11-12), with the build's own decisions named in its commit: the prompts asked before the floor at Publish, a refusal of a prompt being final for the PWA and costing no reviewer call; the refusal's words; and the values a unit is given, built from the type as instantiation and the route build them (`unitSubjectOfType`) and pinned against that code.
    - **What changes, the edit.** The agent's `edit_pwu_type` (`tools.ts`) builds the template it sends from the type's stored template and the prompt fields the call passes: the text (`promptTemplateText`), the output contract (`promptOutputContract`) and the armed state (`promptActive`). A field the call does not pass keeps its stored value; one it passes takes effect. So an edit passing new text alone keeps the stored status and output contract; one passing `promptActive` or an output contract alone changes that field on the stored text. A field counts as passed when the call carries it, as the tool's other fields already do. An empty text is read as not passed, so a call neither makes a template from it nor clears one, as today (the lead's decision). A type with no stored template gets one only from a call that passes a text, DRAFT unless `promptActive` is true, as today. The declared markers are those of the text the template ends with (`markersIn`); its version is the stored template's, and 1 for a new one (the tools set 1 on every template they build today; keeping the stored version is the lead's decision). `define_pwu_type`, `define_from_template` and `scaffold_graph`, which create types, keep today's rule. The tool's description, *"Edit an existing PWU Type in place. Only the fields you pass change."*, is unchanged: the code now does what it says.
    - **What changes, Publish.** `PublishPwa` (`pwa-authoring.ts`) refuses a PWA in which a live type declaring `workDischarge` MODEL has no prompt template, or has one its unit's step would refuse to render: a DRAFT, or one using a marker no work unit of that type supplies. This is decided by the code that decides it when the step runs (`renderPromptTemplate`, `prompt-render.ts`), given the values a unit of that type is given (its title and description from the type, and `{purpose}` only when the type's purpose is not empty; `WORK_UNIT_MARKERS`, `execution-invocation.ts`), so the check and the step cannot disagree (the lead's decision); the build names any value it cannot take from the code that gives it when the step runs. The refusal names each such type and the reason its prompt cannot run, in words the build writes and the commit names; nothing is published and the PWA is left as it was. The check sits in `PublishPwa`, as the sponsor said, not in `ValidatePwa`. `REG-F-353`'s per-edit decision is untouched: a draft may hold a DRAFT prompt, and publishing ends the draft (`REG-F-508`). A refused PWA stays VALIDATED: the publication machine has no arrow back to DRAFT (`PWA.publicationStatus`, `transitions.data.ts`), as for a PWA the floor refuses at Publish today, so its repair is a new PWA; a check at Submit would leave a refused PWA DRAFT, ~~and is not what the sponsor chose (the lead's note).~~ and the sponsor has since ruled it, beside this check (`REG-D-155`; D66).
    - **What the lane does.** Nothing new: half B presses Publish and ends the run `authoring-stopped` on any refused act, its outcome written to `lane/actions/` (`designerEnd`, `phase7-operator.ts`), so a refusal at Publish ends the run at authoring, its words on record, before any unit's press.
    - **What stays.** The engine's per-edit rule: an ACTIVE template only on a MODEL type. The refusals when a step runs (no template; a DRAFT; a marker no one supplies), now a second line behind Publish. The candidate checks, which record and refuse nothing (`REG-D-110` (4)). The agent's system prompt, its tools' descriptions, help strings and results, and every reviewer's prompt, so `REVIEWER_FINGERPRINT_TODAY` does not move.
    - **Where it reaches.** Every PWA's Publish, hand-built and agent-built (`REG-F-508`). Every existing test that publishes a MODEL type whose prompt cannot run is named in the build: one whose subject is something else has its fixture's prompt armed or its markers made supplied, its subject unchanged; one whose subject is a refusal when a step runs reaches it without publishing, or is restated to the refusal at Publish; none is weakened.
    - **Its tests, each red at HEAD.** The fifth run's case: an edit passing a stored ACTIVE prompt's new text and output contract and not `promptActive` keeps it ACTIVE. An edit passing new text alone keeps the stored output contract. An edit passing an output contract alone changes it on the stored text. An edit passing `promptActive: true` alone arms the stored text. Publish refuses, naming the type, a PWA with a MODEL type whose prompt is DRAFT; whose template is absent; whose prompt uses `{criteria}`; and whose prompt uses `{purpose}` while its purpose is empty; nothing published. Controls, green at HEAD and after: an edit passing `promptActive: false` with new text saves it DRAFT; a new type defined without `promptActive` is DRAFT; a PWA whose MODEL types all render publishes, a type declaring HUMAN with no prompt among them. **Its checks.** The ledger's mutants (each field's fallback to its stored value; each of Publish's three conditions; the `{purpose}` rule; the naming of each type); `reviewer:qual:check` GREEN with the fingerprint unchanged; `bun run gate:fast` green, and the full gate, vitest and Playwright.

### 12.20 D66 — Submit refuses a PWA that Validate or Publish would refuse for its structure, its prompts or its floor, while it can still be edited (`REG-D-155`; `REG-F-502`, remedy 1; `REG-F-508`; `REG-F-627`)

66. **D66, DECIDED (2026-11-12) — `SubmitPwaForReview` refuses a PWA whose type structure `ValidatePwa` would refuse, whose model-run prompts `PublishPwa` would refuse, or whose floor `PublishPwa` would find missing or recorded at another version, naming every check that fails, so each refusal comes while the PWA is DRAFT and its types can be edited; `ValidatePwa` and `PublishPwa` keep their own checks; a paid run that Submit refuses stops, as on any refused act.** This is the lead's design of `REG-D-155`. The lead's decisions in it, each named: each check is the one that decides it later, called and not spelled again; one refusal names every check that fails, in the order a person repairs them; and the refusal's words, which the build writes and its commit names. ~~The build is OWED (package L11), after P6 again's sixth run has ended.~~ Built in package L11 (`7e45efb0`, 2026-11-12), with the build's own decisions named in its commit: the refusal's code and words; a later state a test needs, reached by writing the PWA's status as a store from before package L11 holds it, with no event; the waiver spec's second, mock, agent turn, so Publish's own check is reached there through real commands; and the floor spec read hop by hop, for a mutant whose run had timed out.
    - **What changes, Submit.** `SubmitPwaForReview` (`pwa-authoring.ts`), which has a state precondition and no guard today, gains a guard after its precondition (`fromStates('DRAFT')`). It asks three things, each decided by the code that decides it later, so Submit and the later check cannot disagree (the lead's decision): (1) **the structure**, as `ValidatePwa` asks it (`pwaCompositionGate`: `analyzePwaGraph` over `pwaGraphExport`) — exactly one root, no composition cycle, every type reachable from the root (§11.6); (2) **the prompts**, as `PublishPwa` asks them since package L10 (`pwaPromptGate`: each live type declaring MODEL whose unit's step could not render its prompt, `unitPromptRefusal`; D65); (3) **the floor**, as `PublishPwa` asks it (`pwaFloorGate`: `pwaPartsFloorBlock`, for a PWA an agent produced or one with a recorded floor) — the PWA's two deterministic assessments at its version and each live type's review at that type's version, so a floor missing, or recorded before a type's edit, is refused, and a PWA no agent produced and for which no floor was recorded passes, as at Publish.
    - **The refusal.** One refusal names every check that fails, each with the detail its own check gives (the structure's failed invariants; each type and why its prompt cannot run; each floor policy's disposition), the structure and the prompts first and the floor last, since repairing either is a type's edit and an edit leaves a recorded floor at another version (the lead's decision). For the floor it names the remedy that exists while the PWA is DRAFT: an agent turn in the PWA Designer, which records one (`runPwaFloor`, called only by the agent route; `REG-F-502`). Nothing is submitted: the PWA stays DRAFT, where its types can be edited, which is the point of the ruling. The words are the build's (the lead's decision), named in its commit.
    - **What stays.** `ValidatePwa`'s composition check and `PublishPwa`'s prompt and floor checks, each with its words, as a second guard (`REG-D-155` (2)). The per-edit rule, and `REG-F-353`'s decision that a draft may hold any prompt: the check comes when the draft is submitted. The candidate checks, which record and refuse nothing (`REG-D-110` (4)). The page, whose Submit button is disabled as today and shows the refusal as it shows any refused act. `REG-F-502`'s remedies 2 and 3, not ruled.
    - **What the lane does.** Nothing new (`REG-D-155` (3)): half B presses Submit and ends the run `authoring-stopped` on a refused act, its outcome recorded (`designerEnd`, `phase7-operator.ts`), the PWA left DRAFT. No turn is added.
    - **Who reads the refusal.** A person, on the page whose Submit button issues the command (`routes/pwa/[id]/+page.server.ts`), and the lane. No agent tool issues `SubmitPwaForReview`, so no model reads it: the agent's system prompt, its tools' descriptions, help strings and results, and every reviewer's prompt are unchanged, and `REVIEWER_FINGERPRINT_TODAY` does not move.
    - **Where it reaches.** Every PWA's Submit, hand-built and agent-built. The replay (`replay-pwa.ts`) re-creates a recorded PWA through the engine's commands, with no floor, and submits, validates and publishes it — a half-B recording among its regression's cases (`replay-regression.test.ts:839`) — so Publish's floor check passes it today; Submit asks the same rule, and a recording that published has a structure Validate passed and, since package L10, prompts Publish passed, so it is not refused at Submit; a recording that package L10's check refuses at Publish, the fifth run's among them, is refused at Submit instead. Every existing test that submits a PWA Submit now refuses is named in the build: one whose subject is something else has its fixture made to pass the three checks, its subject unchanged; one whose subject is a later check's refusal (`ValidatePwa`'s, or `PublishPwa`'s prompt or floor refusal, package L10's among them) is restated to the refusal at Submit, or reaches the later check without Submit's guard where the build can arrange that, the later check kept under a test; none is weakened.
    - **Its tests, each red at HEAD.** Submit refuses, the PWA left DRAFT, naming the failure: two roots; a composition cycle; an unreachable type; a MODEL type whose prompt is DRAFT, whose template is absent, or whose prompt uses `{criteria}`; an agent-produced PWA with no floor recorded; one whose floor a type's edit has left at another version. A PWA failing two or three checks is refused once, each named, in the order above. After a refusal the PWA is DRAFT: an edit of a named type is accepted, and Submit, asked again, refuses for what remains — for an agent-produced PWA, its floor, now at another version — the fifth run's case carried to its repair. In half B's rehearsal with the mock agent, a MODEL leaf left DRAFT ends the run `authoring-stopped` at Submit, the PWA DRAFT. Controls, green at HEAD and after: a PWA no agent produced, with no floor recorded, a valid structure and runnable prompts, submits; an agent-produced PWA whose floor is current submits, validates and publishes; `ValidatePwa`'s and `PublishPwa`'s refusals are unchanged, byte for byte, wherever they are reached. **Its checks.** The ledger's mutants (each of Submit's three checks removed; each one's condition; the naming of every failing check; the order; the PWA left DRAFT); `reviewer:qual:check` GREEN with the fingerprint unchanged; `bun run gate:fast` green, and the full gate, vitest and Playwright.

### 12.21 D67 — the refinement asks for each finding's problem wherever it occurs, and one more refinement and review follow when findings remain (`REG-D-156`; `REG-F-628`; `REG-F-629`)

67. **D67, DECIDED (2026-11-12) — the refinement's directive asks the agent to look for each finding's problem in every type and fix it wherever it occurs; and when the second round still holds findings asking for revision, the agent is given the directive again and every type is reviewed a third time.** The sponsor's ruling (`REG-D-156`); the words, the count and the reach below are the lead's, labelled.
    - **Why.** P6 again's sixth paid run stopped in its authoring (`REG-F-628`): the first round rejected one evidence leaf for a gap its sibling shared, the one refinement repaired the named type alone, as the directive asks, and the second round rejected the unchanged sibling and judged another unchanged type INCONCLUSIVE, with no refinement left.
    - **What changes, the directive (the lead's words).** `refineDirective` (`apps/rph-demo/src/lib/server/floor.ts`) gains two sentences, after *"Revise the PWA graph and/or its recorded professional rationale as each coded finding requires; do not merely restate the intent:"* and before the findings: Every type of the PWA is reviewed again after this revision, not only those named below. Where a finding names a problem, look for the same problem in every other type and fix it wherever it occurs. Nothing else in it changes: the findings by type, the failed parts' clause, the original intent.
    - **What changes, the rounds (the lead's count).** The agent route (`apps/rph-demo/src/routes/pwa/[id]/agent/+server.ts`) allows two refinements. After the second round, when its remediation asks for refinement by the rule that asked for the first (`classifyFloorRemediation`'s `autoRefine`), the agent is given the directive with the second round's findings and failed parts, and the floor runs a third time: the whole part inventory again, under a new round token and the candidate's new hash. After the third round the turn ends as it ends today, its guidance shown when the floor is not satisfied. No third refinement. A satisfied round, a round with no review, a PWA with no type and a round the allowance stopped end the loop as they end it today.
    - **What the third round's reviewer is shown.** Each part, its own type's findings from the round before (`priorFindings`), as the second round is shown the first's: the same construct, so no reviewer prompt's form changes and the reviewer's fingerprint does not move.
    - **The lane.** `PHASE7_AUTHORING_FLOOR_RUNS` (`apps/rph-demo/e2e-live-exec/lane-config.ts`) becomes 3, so the authoring turn's time allowance and its worst-case reviewer calls count three floor runs. Each round's reservation is unchanged (`REG-F-580`; D20): a third round reserves its types × 6 against what is left of the allowance before its first call, or calls none. At the seventh run's prior calls, 3,665, the authoring review is handed 931; three rounds of 18 types reserve 324.
    - **What stays.** The candidate's status rules, the guidance's words, the page, the per-edit rule, D66's checks at Submit, and every reviewer prompt's text. Mock mode still runs no refinement.
    - **Who reads it.** The agent reads the directive; no reviewer does. Before the seventh run, the materialized directive, rendered by the product's own function over the sixth run's first-round findings, is shown to the sponsor. ⭑ Shown 2026-11-13, before the seventh run (`p6again7-facts.md` §4), and found byte for byte the same at `4e05ee0d` (§6).
    - **Its tests, each red at HEAD.** The directive holds the two sentences in their place, the rest byte for byte; a turn whose second round asks for refinement runs a second refinement and a third round, the third shown the second's findings; a turn whose second round is satisfied, stopped by the allowance, or failed operationally alone runs no third; no turn runs a fourth round; the lane's allowance counts three floor runs. Controls, green at HEAD and after: a turn whose first round is satisfied runs one round; mock mode runs no refinement. Mutants: each sentence removed; the second refinement removed; a third refinement allowed; the third round shown the first round's findings; the lane's count left at 2.
    - **Not in it.** The reviewer's two verdicts on one gap (`REG-F-629`): no remedy ruled.
    - ⭑ **Amended 2026-11-12 (the lead's, found by package L12's builder).** The authoring agent's system prompt (`apps/rph-demo/src/lib/server/agent/system-prompt.ts`, GOVERNANCE) said that a valid finding of the review *"may bring you back once, in the same turn, to revise the candidate"*; with two refinements it says at most twice, that one word replaced and nothing else in it changing: a text the agent reads, beside the directive, which package L12 changes and the sponsor is shown before the seventh run. And of the tests above, three cannot be red at HEAD, which never runs a third round: a turn whose second round is satisfied, stopped by the allowance, or failed operationally alone runs no third. They are guards, green at HEAD, each killed by its stopping-rule mutant. ⭑ Shown 2026-11-13 with the directive, before the seventh run (`p6again7-facts.md` §4 and §6).
    - ⭑ **Amended 2026-11-12.** Nor is a review the floor holds INCONCLUSIVE for a failed check, whose findings no refinement carries (`REG-F-630`): that is D68's (`REG-D-157`).
    - ⭑ **Built 2026-11-13** in package L12 (`230574f3`), with the build's own decisions named in its commit; after the gate, the lead's review restored one assertion that a restated test had made conditional.

### 12.22 D68 — a review the floor holds INCONCLUSIVE for a failed check is answered as a rejected one, in the authoring and in a unit's review (`REG-D-157`; `REG-F-630`; `REG-D-059`)

68. **D68, DECIDED (2026-11-12) — a reasoning review that the floor holds INCONCLUSIVE and that failed a check the review declares is answered as a REJECTED review is: its failed checks go back to the producer, the authoring agent's repair and the next round's reviewer, or a unit's executor for another try; and a round held so asks for a repair.** The sponsor's ruling (`REG-D-157`); the test, the reach, the bounds and the templates left unchanged, below, are the lead's, labelled.
    - **Why.** In P6 again's sixth paid run, a type unchanged between the rounds was recommended SATISFIED in the second with one check failed; the floor held it INCONCLUSIVE, and the classifier sends no INCONCLUSIVE review's observations back, so no repair could carry its check, and alone it would have ended the turn with no repair (`REG-F-630`). A unit's review held so gets no further try. Package L12's second refinement asks the same rule.
    - **What changes (the lead's test).** In `classifyFloorRemediation` (`apps/rph-demo/src/lib/server/floor.ts`), a review whose disposition is INCONCLUSIVE and which carries an open observation whose code is one of the checks the reasoning review declares (`REASONING_REVIEW_CRITERIA`, `packages/rph-assurance/src/validators.ts`) is answered as a REJECTED review is: those observations are its findings. For a review in parts, each such part's failed checks join the findings beside those of its REJECTED and CONDITIONALLY_SATISFIED parts; for a unit's review, which has no parts, the review's own. ~~A round whose folded review is INCONCLUSIVE and which has such findings is answered REFINE_SUBJECT, `autoRefine` true, with REFINE_SUBJECT's guidance.~~ A round in which some review is held so, and which has such findings, is answered REFINE_SUBJECT, `autoRefine` true, with REFINE_SUBJECT's guidance, whatever its fold, as a round with a REJECTED review is (amended 2026-11-13, `REG-D-158`; `REG-F-632`). The record keeps no reviewer recommendation but ESCALATED (`REG-F-475`), so the test is the record's own: a declared check failed. A reviewer that returned INCONCLUSIVE itself and failed a declared check is therefore answered the same way, since it named a problem: the lead's reading of the sponsor's *"A review that really couldn't tell still sends nothing back."*, named here for the sponsor.
    - **Where the findings go.** Where a REJECTED review's go today: the authoring directive (`refineDirective`, under their type) and the next round's reviewer (`priorFindings`, narrowed to each part's type); a unit's next try and a stopped unit's recovery offers, as REFINE_SUBJECT is answered there.
    - **What stays.** An INCONCLUSIVE review with no failed declared check (an unanswered check, an operational failure, a boundary-rejected reply, a code no check declares) sends nothing back, as `REG-D-059`'s note asks. Every earlier branch of the classifier is asked first, as today. The kernel's foreclosure, `R25` and the recorded dispositions are unchanged: the review stays INCONCLUSIVE in the record, and the floor blocks until a later review passes. Package L12's two refinements and three rounds, a unit's bound on its tries and each round's reservation (`REG-F-580`) are unchanged; a repair or a try D68 brings counts within them. No template a model reads changes, so the reviewer's fingerprint does not move. The PWA page's blocked hint follows the remediation's action, so for such a round it reads the hint for a revision; no code there changes.
    - **Its tests, each red at HEAD.** The sixth run's second-round shape: the INCONCLUSIVE type's failed check is among the findings, and in the directive beside the REJECTED type's; the INCONCLUSIVE type alone: REFINE_SUBJECT, `autoRefine` true, its check listed; the agent route runs a repair for a round held so, and the next round's reviewer is shown the type's check; a unit's review held so: another try, its check among the executor's findings. Controls, green at HEAD and after: an INCONCLUSIVE review with no failed declared check (an unanswered check, an operational code, a boundary-rejected reply, a code no check declares) sends nothing and is answered as today; a satisfied round; a REJECTED round. Mutants: the INCONCLUSIVE arm removed; any code taken for a declared check; the folded INCONCLUSIVE left unanswered; the unit's review left out.
    - **Not in it.** Recording every reviewer recommendation (`REG-F-475`); the reviewer's two verdicts on one gap (`REG-F-629`).
    - ⭑ **Built 2026-11-13** in package L13 (`769394ba`), with the build's own decisions named in its commit, among them: the composition records a reply that itself recommends INCONCLUSIVE, or ESCALATED, over a failed check REJECTED, so in the product the review this design holds is the boundary's case alone, a SATISFIED recommendation over a failed check, the sixth run's; and the command evidence's review shares the classifier, so it is reached too.
    - ⭑ **Amended 2026-11-13 (`REG-D-158`; `REG-F-632`; the lead's review of package L13).** *"A round whose folded review is INCONCLUSIVE"*, the lead's words above, left one corner: the fold reads REJECTED if any part is REJECTED, else UNASSESSED if any part's review is missing or not concluded, else INCONCLUSIVE (`partsDisposition`, `aggregateDisposition`), so beside a part the round holds no concluded review of, a held part was answered as an incomplete review where a REJECTED part is answered by revision. Met only by the PWA page's read, never by the authoring route or a unit's review. The sponsor ruled it fixed before the seventh run (`REG-D-158`). The lead's decisions, labelled: a round in which some review is held so, with such findings, is answered as a round with a REJECTED review is, whatever its fold; a review that could not tell, beside such a part, is answered as before; the fold shown and the recorded dispositions are unchanged; `P6X-L13-M11`'s mutation becomes the product, abolished by `REG-D-158`. And the lead's reading of the answer: the seventh run follows the build without another question, since nothing a model reads changes. Its tests: package L13's control for a held part beside a part with no review, restated, red at `769394ba`; controls, green at `769394ba` and after: a REJECTED part beside such a part, and a part that could not tell beside one. Package L14.
    - ⭑ **Amendment built 2026-11-13** in package L14 (`4e05ee0d`), with the build's own decisions named in its commit, among them: a type edited after its round keeps its version and is read with its round's review, so the page-read reds are built over a type added since the round and over a newer request under the round's token (`REG-F-637`); and the beside-failed-reviews branch and the escalation note move with the rule, each pinned.

### 12.23 D69 — a unit is given the accepted work of the units it depends on, by a file and a pointer (`REG-D-159`; `REG-F-634`; `REG-F-358`)

69. **D69, DECIDED (2026-11-13) — when a unit is accepted, the workbench writes its accepted work product to a known file in the Undertaking's shared workspace; and each unit whose recorded dependencies name it is told, at the end of its instruction, which files hold that work and to read them before it begins.** The sponsor's ruling (`REG-D-159`); the moment, the bytes, the place, the section's words and seat, the refresh, the refusal and the context policy's update are the lead's, labelled.
    - **Why.** In P6 again's seventh paid run the Technical Design never saw the accepted Requirements Specification, though the engine started it only once the Specification was accepted (D1, D2). Begin & Execute binds no inputs (`apps/rph-demo/src/routes/undertakings/[id]/+page.server.ts:2796-2812`, `inputBindings: []`), a unit's prompt carries only its own fields (`packages/rph-application/src/execution-invocation.ts:255-292`), and the executor's loader takes nothing from the folder; so the only hand-off is files a unit happens to leave in the shared workspace (`REG-F-358`, corrected 2026-10-09), and the Specification left none. The design chose otherwise on two points the Specification had settled, and the composite unit's declared reviews rejected it (`REG-F-634`). Canon `DEC-4`: *"a context omission must not silently remove a mandatory constraint"*.
    - **When it is written (the lead's).** When Mark Satisfied moves a unit into SATISFIED, the workbench writes the unit's file once the state change commits. Mark Satisfied is the page's one way to accept a unit; RECOMPOSED and BASELINED are entered only after SATISFIED, and the page offers neither. A write that fails is stated on the page and does not undo the acceptance. And before a unit's Begin & Execute, and before a work re-run, the workbench writes again, from the record, the file of each unit it depends on, before the unit's workspace baseline is taken: so the unit starts on the accepted bytes even if a command since changed or removed a file in the shared folder, and the files are never counted as its own change.
    - **What it holds (the lead's).** The accepted work product, byte for byte: the bytes of the work product the unit's last successful step completion names, the one the page's sign-off reconstructs and its declared reviews judged (the sign-off's own selector, reused), read from the content store and checked against the artifact's `contentHash` before it is written, as the stopped-unit recovery reads one (`apps/rph-demo/src/lib/server/execution/stopped-unit-recovery.ts:834-840`). That is the reply's `workProduct` part as stored, JSON where the unit returned an object; not the rationale and not the raw reply. A unit whose last successful completion names no work product has no file.
    - **Where (the lead's).** `.jpwb/accepted/<slug>--<unit id>.txt` in the Undertaking's shared workspace, written through the workspace port (`Workspace.writeFile`, `packages/rph-ports/src/ports/workspace.ts:128`) and its containment checks. `<slug>` is the unit's title in lower case, each run of characters other than `a` to `z` and `0` to `9` one hyphen, trimmed of hyphens, at most 60 characters; the unit's id makes the name unique. The files stay in the folder, which is the deliverable (`R8`), as the workbench's record of what each unit was given.
    - **Who is told (the lead's reading of the sponsor's *"each unit that depends on it"*).** Each unit whose recorded dependencies (`dependencyIds`, D1) name the accepted unit: the producers of its required inputs and, for a parent, its children. Direct dependencies only; a dependency with no file is not listed.
    - **The section (the lead's words and seat).** Last in the instruction, after the output contract, the line `This work depends on the accepted work of other units of this Undertaking. Before you begin, read each file below. Each holds one unit's accepted work product, exactly as its reviews judged it. The workbench writes these files; do not change them.`, then one line per listed dependency, in the order of `dependencyIds`: `- <its title>: <its file>`. A unit with none gets no section, absent meaning absent, as D61's. The seat is last because the authoring candidate checks test whether the type's text, the Undertaking's statement and the output contract fit the 4,000 characters a reviewer is shown of an instruction (`PROMPT-LENGTH`; `USER_INTENT_MAX`): that part is shown as today, and the section after it as far as it fits.
    - **The refusal (the lead's).** If a dependency's accepted work cannot be read, its bytes fail their hash, or its file cannot be written, the unit's Begin & Execute or work re-run refuses before any model or reviewer call, naming the dependency and the reason, and nothing of the unit moves.
    - **What the step declares (the lead's, as `REG-F-358` asks).** A step whose instruction carries the section declares that it assembles prior work products at the Undertaking's scope (`PRIOR_WORK_PRODUCT` at `THIS_UNDERTAKING`, in `alsoAssembles`), and the seeded `ctx-default` permits that pair; a policy that does not refuses the invocation before the render (`CP-04`'s ceiling). `REG-F-358`: *"the day it does, the policy is wrong until someone updates it."*
    - **What stays.** The order and its gate (D1, D2, D6); the executor's tools, `write_file` and `run_command`; a held session's repair turn, which sends the findings and not the instruction again; the reviewers' templates, so the reviewer's fingerprint does not move; Half A's instructions, since its leaves record no dependency and its root has no instruction; and the lane's worst case for each press.
    - **Who reads it.** The executor reads the section; the reviews shown the producing instruction see it as far as it fits (the work-product floor, each command's review, a leaf's declared reviews); a composite's declared reviews, shown its purpose, do not. Before the eighth run, the materialized sections of the seventh run's PWA's units, rendered by the product's own code, are shown to the sponsor.
    - **Its tests, each red at HEAD.** Mark Satisfied on a unit with a work product writes its file, hash-equal to the artifact; a unit that depends on an accepted unit is given the section, as sent and as a reviewer is shown it, naming the dependency's title and file, and the file holds the accepted bytes before the executor opens and is not in the unit's deliverable view; a dependency's file changed or removed after its acceptance is written again before the unit starts; a dependency whose stored bytes are missing or fail their hash refuses the start before any model call, naming it; a work re-run carries the section; the stopped-unit recovery's reconstruction renders the same instruction; the step declares the pair, `ctx-default` permits it, and a policy without it refuses before the render. Controls, green at HEAD and after: a unit with no dependency, its instruction byte for byte today's; Half A's instructions unchanged; the reviewer's fingerprint unmoved.
    - **Not in it.** Showing a review the content of a unit's inputs, or a composite's review its children's work whole (`REG-F-636`); the Unit Test Suite accepted with no test run (`REG-F-635`); which unit writes the product's own documents, which the PWA's prompts decide; dependencies of dependencies.
